What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on her hikes.\n- Chief Complaint: “Out on the trails and even back in town, I keep hearing and sensing things from the landscape telling me what to do with my work, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the mountains around Bozeman chose me for some bigger artistic mission, like they’re grooming me to be this famous visionary choreographer who will ‘wake people up,’ and no matter how many bills I miss or how stressed my partner gets, it still feels absolutely true and not like something I could be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling me things like which dancers to fire, which festivals to skip because they’re ‘beneath my purpose,’ and even what colors to paint the studio walls, and it feels so real that I’ve started avoiding certain places because I’m scared of what it will tell me next.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): After those weeks of feeling unstoppable, I’ve crashed into this heavy, gray place where I wake up with my chest already tight and it’s like someone flipped a switch and drained all the joy out of dancing and running the studio, and I just sit on my couch staring at old rehearsal videos and crying without really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my appetite swung the other way and I started mindlessly eating cereal or takeout straight from the container because cooking or even thinking about real food feels like too much work.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): For weeks I hardly slept at all, staying up until dawn rearranging choreography, answering imaginary ‘cosmic emails’ from the mountains, and then lately I’ve been either lying awake all night replaying every mistake I’ve made with the studio or sleeping until the afternoon and still waking up feeling like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body has been all over the place—during the intense phase I was pacing circles around the studio, rearranging props and running combinations over and over until my dancers begged me to stop, and now I move so slowly that even walking from my bed to the kitchen feels like I’m dragging through wet cement.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I don’t teach or rehearse, I feel utterly wiped out, like my muscles are made of sandbags and my brain is fogged over, and just answering a simple text from a student about class times can exhaust me enough that I have to lie back down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When the mood dropped, I started thinking that all the chaos with the studio—missed grants, dancers quitting, my partner almost walking away—is proof that I’m a selfish fraud who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’ve been riding this wild swing where one week I’m so amped up and euphoric that I’m blasting music at 3 a.m., planning ten new projects and talking a mile a minute about my ‘movement revolution,’ and the next week the smallest thing—like a student asking about a refund—sets me off into this sharp, hot anger or sends me spiraling into tears.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During the high stretch I couldn’t sit still for five minutes, so I’d be choreographing three pieces at once, calling old contacts out of the blue, booking festivals without checking dates, and then driving out to trailheads at midnight to ‘get fresh inspiration’ because I felt like I physically couldn’t stop moving or creating.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I got so convinced of my own brilliance that I told my partner we didn’t need a real business plan because my ‘genius’ alone would attract investors and sell-out shows, and I honestly believed I was on the verge of becoming this internationally famous choreographer who would put Bozeman on the global dance map.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): People keep telling me they can’t get a word in because once I start talking about my ideas—how the mountains are choreographing through me, how I’m going to reinvent modern dance—I just keep going faster and faster, jumping from one concept to the next without stopping to breathe or even noticing that they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt this restless drive to just keep doing something.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_manic_episode: Those voices from the mountains and the unshakable feeling that I’d been chosen for some huge artistic mission only showed up during that revved‑up stretch when I was barely sleeping and buzzing with ideas, and once I crashed into this low, gray phase they stopped even though the depression kept going.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my studio is hanging by a thread—I’ve lost students, missed grant deadlines and payments, my business partner is talking about backing out, and I’m so overwhelmed and withdrawn now that I can’t reliably run classes, answer emails, or keep up with basic chores at home.\n- duration: All of this—the wild high with the studio chaos and then this long, heavy crash—has been going on since I signed the lease and launched the studio full-time earlier this year, so it’s been about nine months altogether.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The low, exhausted, guilty feeling really settled in a couple of months after the studio launch and has dragged on for around nine months now without really lifting.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on her hikes.\n- Chief Complaint: “Out on the trails and even back in town, I keep hearing and sensing things from the landscape telling me what to do with my work, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ to be this famous visionary choreographer who will ‘wake people up,’ and no matter how many bills I miss or how stressed my partner gets, it still feels absolutely true and not like something I could be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my (Sleep_Disturbance): For weeks I hardly slept at all, staying up until dawn rearranging choreography, answering imaginary ‘cosmic emails’ from the mountains, and then lately I’ve been either lying who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt I also haven't had another one of those revved-up phases since then. I've just been stuck in this really heavy, low place for the last several months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week where you felt unusually energized or irritable, needed very little sleep, talked more than usual, had racing thoughts, or did risky things, even if you did not hear voices then?\n\nPatient: Yes, I had a highly energized period just like that when I first launched the studio earlier this year. I felt completely unstoppable, barely slept for weeks, talked constantly about my ideas, and was incredibly active. But I actually did hear those voices during that exact same high-energy phase, not separate from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations or unusual beliefs when your mood was not high or low, or do they only happen during those energized or depressed periods?\n\nPatient: No, they have never happened when my mood was normal. The voices and the feeling of having a special mission only occurred during that highly energized, revved-up period when I wasn't sleeping. Once I crashed into this low, depressed phase, those experiences stopped completely."

What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on her hikes.\n- Chief Complaint: “Out on the trails and even back in town, I keep hearing and sensing things from the landscape telling me what to do with my work, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the mountains around Bozeman chose me for some bigger artistic mission, like they’re grooming me to be this famous visionary choreographer who will ‘wake people up,’ and no matter how many bills I miss or how stressed my partner gets, it still feels absolutely true and not like something I could be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling me things like which dancers to fire, which festivals to skip because they’re ‘beneath my purpose,’ and even what colors to paint the studio walls, and it feels so real that I’ve started avoiding certain places because I’m scared of what it will tell me next.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): After those weeks of feeling unstoppable, I’ve crashed into this heavy, gray place where I wake up with my chest already tight and it’s like someone flipped a switch and drained all the joy out of dancing and running the studio, and I just sit on my couch staring at old rehearsal videos and crying without really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my appetite swung the other way and I started mindlessly eating cereal or takeout straight from the container because cooking or even thinking about real food feels like too much work.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): For weeks I hardly slept at all, staying up until dawn rearranging choreography, answering imaginary ‘cosmic emails’ from the mountains, and then lately I’ve been either lying awake all night replaying every mistake I’ve made with the studio or sleeping until the afternoon and still waking up feeling like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body has been all over the place—during the intense phase I was pacing circles around the studio, rearranging props and running combinations over and over until my dancers begged me to stop, and now I move so slowly that even walking from my bed to the kitchen feels like I’m dragging through wet cement.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I don’t teach or rehearse, I feel utterly wiped out, like my muscles are made of sandbags and my brain is fogged over, and just answering a simple text from a student about class times can exhaust me enough that I have to lie back down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When the mood dropped, I started thinking that all the chaos with the studio—missed grants, dancers quitting, my partner almost walking away—is proof that I’m a selfish fraud who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’ve been riding this wild swing where one week I’m so amped up and euphoric that I’m blasting music at 3 a.m., planning ten new projects and talking a mile a minute about my ‘movement revolution,’ and the next week the smallest thing—like a student asking about a refund—sets me off into this sharp, hot anger or sends me spiraling into tears.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During the high stretch I couldn’t sit still for five minutes, so I’d be choreographing three pieces at once, calling old contacts out of the blue, booking festivals without checking dates, and then driving out to trailheads at midnight to ‘get fresh inspiration’ because I felt like I physically couldn’t stop moving or creating.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I got so convinced of my own brilliance that I told my partner we didn’t need a real business plan because my ‘genius’ alone would attract investors and sell-out shows, and I honestly believed I was on the verge of becoming this internationally famous choreographer who would put Bozeman on the global dance map.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): People keep telling me they can’t get a word in because once I start talking about my ideas—how the mountains are choreographing through me, how I’m going to reinvent modern dance—I just keep going faster and faster, jumping from one concept to the next without stopping to breathe or even noticing that they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt this restless drive to just keep doing something.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_manic_episode: Those voices from the mountains and the unshakable feeling that I’d been chosen for some huge artistic mission only showed up during that revved‑up stretch when I was barely sleeping and buzzing with ideas, and once I crashed into this low, gray phase they stopped even though the depression kept going.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my studio is hanging by a thread—I’ve lost students, missed grant deadlines and payments, my business partner is talking about backing out, and I’m so overwhelmed and withdrawn now that I can’t reliably run classes, answer emails, or keep up with basic chores at home.\n- Once I crashed into this low, depressed phase, those experiences stopped completely.\n\nDoctor: How long did that revved-up, energized period last—was it at least 1 week, or did it go on for 4 days or less?\n\nPatient: Oh, it lasted much longer than just a week or a few days. It actually went on for about two months of constant, intense activity before I finally hit a wall and crashed. It was weeks and weeks of feeling completely wired and barely sleeping.\n\nDoctor: During that two-month energized period, did you also have any times of feeling very depressed or losing interest in things for at least two weeks, or was it only the high-energy phase followed by the current low phase?\n\nPatient: No, those first two months were purely that high-energy, unstoppable phase. It was only after that period ended that I crashed into this heavy, depressed state, which has dragged on for about nine months now without lifting."

What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on her hikes.\n- Chief Complaint: “Out on the trails and even back in town, I keep hearing and sensing things from the landscape telling me what to do with my work, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the mountains around Bozeman chose me for some bigger artistic mission, like they’re grooming me to be this famous visionary choreographer who will ‘wake people up,’ and no matter how many bills I miss or how stressed my partner gets, it still feels absolutely true and not like something I could be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling me things like which dancers to fire, which festivals to skip because they’re ‘beneath my purpose,’ and even what colors to paint the studio walls, and it feels so real that I’ve started avoiding certain places because I’m scared of what it will tell me next.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): After those weeks of feeling unstoppable, I’ve crashed into this heavy, gray place where I wake up with my chest already tight and it’s like someone flipped a switch and drained all the joy out of dancing and running the studio, and I just sit on my couch staring at old rehearsal videos and crying without really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my appetite swung the other way and I started mindlessly eating cereal or takeout straight from the container because cooking or even thinking about real food feels like too much work.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): For weeks I hardly slept at all, staying up until dawn rearranging choreography, answering imaginary ‘cosmic emails’ from the mountains, and then lately I’ve been either lying awake all night replaying every mistake I’ve made with the studio or sleeping until the afternoon and still waking up feeling like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body has been all over the place—during the intense phase I was pacing circles around the studio, rearranging props and running combinations over and over until my dancers begged me to stop, and now I move so slowly that even walking from my bed to the kitchen feels like I’m dragging through wet cement.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I don’t teach or rehearse, I feel utterly wiped out, like my muscles are made of sandbags and my brain is fogged over, and just answering a simple text from a student about class times can exhaust me enough that I have to lie back down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When the mood dropped, I started thinking that all the chaos with the studio—missed grants, dancers quitting, my partner almost walking away—is proof that I’m a selfish fraud who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’ve been riding this wild swing where one week I’m so amped up and euphoric that I’m blasting music at 3 a.m., planning ten new projects and talking a mile a minute about my ‘movement revolution,’ and the next week the smallest thing—like a student asking about a refund—sets me off into this sharp, hot anger or sends me spiraling into tears.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During the high stretch I couldn’t sit still for five minutes, so I’d be choreographing three pieces at once, calling old contacts out of the blue, booking festivals without checking dates, and then driving out to trailheads at midnight to ‘get fresh inspiration’ because I felt like I physically couldn’t stop moving or creating.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I got so convinced of my own brilliance that I told my partner we didn’t need a real business plan because my ‘genius’ alone would attract investors and sell-out shows, and I honestly believed I was on the verge of becoming this internationally famous choreographer who would put Bozeman on the global dance map.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): People keep telling me they can’t get a word in because once I start talking about my ideas—how the mountains are choreographing through me, how I’m going to reinvent modern dance—I just keep going faster and faster, jumping from one concept to the next without stopping to breathe or even noticing that they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt this restless drive to just keep doing something.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_manic_episode: Those voices from the mountains and the unshakable feeling that I’d been chosen for some huge artistic mission only showed up during that revved‑up stretch when I was barely sleeping and buzzing with ideas, and once I crashed into this low, gray phase they stopped even though the depression kept going.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my studio is hanging by a thread—I’ve lost students, missed grant deadlines and payments, my business partner is talking about backing out, and I’m so overwhelmed and withdrawn now that I can’t reliably run classes, answer emails, or keep up with basic chores at home.\n- duration: All of this—the wild high with the studio chaos and then this long, heavy crash—has been going on since I signed the lease and launched the studio full-time earlier this year, so it’s been It was only after that period ended that I crashed into this heavy, depressed state, which has dragged on for about nine months now without lifting.\n\nDoctor: During that two-month high-energy period, did you have any severe problems like needing to be hospitalized, losing touch with reality, or being unable to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but I absolutely lost touch with reality, hearing voices from the mountains and being convinced I was chosen for some special artistic mission. What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on her hikes.\n- Chief Complaint: “Out on the trails and even back in town, I keep hearing and sensing things from the landscape telling me what to do with my work, and it’s starting to scare me.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S019 (Delusions): Lately I’ve been completely convinced that the mountains around Bozeman chose me for some bigger artistic mission, like they’re grooming me to be this famous visionary choreographer who will ‘wake people up,’ and no matter how many bills I miss or how stressed my partner gets, it still feels absolutely true and not like something I could be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling me things like which dancers to fire, which festivals to skip because they’re ‘beneath my purpose,’ and even what colors to paint the studio walls, and it feels so real that I’ve started avoiding certain places because I’m scared of what it will tell me next.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): After those weeks of feeling unstoppable, I’ve crashed into this heavy, gray place where I wake up with my chest already tight and it’s like someone flipped a switch and drained all the joy out of dancing and running the studio, and I just sit on my couch staring at old rehearsal videos and crying without really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my appetite swung the other way and I started mindlessly eating cereal or takeout straight from the container because cooking or even thinking about real food feels like too much work.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): For weeks I hardly slept at all, staying up until dawn rearranging choreography, answering imaginary ‘cosmic emails’ from the mountains, and then lately I’ve been either lying awake all night replaying every mistake I’ve made with the studio or sleeping until the afternoon and still waking up feeling like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body has been all over the place—during the intense phase I was pacing circles around the studio, rearranging props and running combinations over and over until my dancers begged me to stop, and now I move so slowly that even walking from my bed to the kitchen feels like I’m dragging through wet cement.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I don’t teach or rehearse, I feel utterly wiped out, like my muscles are made of sandbags and my brain is fogged over, and just answering a simple text from a student about class times can exhaust me enough that I have to lie back down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When the mood dropped, I started thinking that all the chaos with the studio—missed grants, dancers quitting, my partner almost walking away—is proof that I’m a selfish fraud who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’ve been riding this wild swing where one week I’m so amped up and euphoric that I’m blasting music at 3 a.m., planning ten new projects and talking a mile a minute about my ‘movement revolution,’ and the next week the smallest thing—like a student asking about a refund—sets me off into this sharp, hot anger or sends me spiraling into tears.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During the high stretch I couldn’t sit still for five minutes, so I’d be choreographing three pieces at once, calling old contacts out of the blue, booking festivals without checking dates, and then driving out to trailheads at midnight to ‘get fresh inspiration’ because I felt like I physically couldn’t stop moving or creating.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): I got so convinced of my own brilliance that I told my partner we didn’t need a real business plan because my ‘genius’ alone would attract investors and sell-out shows, and I honestly believed I was on the verge of becoming this internationally famous choreographer who would put Bozeman on the global dance map.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): People keep telling me they can’t get a word in because once I start talking about my ideas—how the mountains are choreographing through me, how I’m going to reinvent modern dance—I just keep going faster and faster, jumping from one concept to the next without stopping to breathe or even noticing that they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt this restless drive to just keep doing something.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_manic_episode: Those voices from the mountains and the unshakable feeling that I’d been chosen for some huge artistic mission only showed up during that revved‑up stretch when I was barely sleeping and buzzing with ideas, and once I crashed into this low, gray phase they stopped even though the depression kept going.\n- functional_impairment: Because of all this, my studio is hanging by a thread—I’ve lost students, missed grant deadlines and payments, my business partner is talking about backing out, and I’m so overwhelmed and withdrawn now that I can’t reliably run classes, answer emails, or keep up with basic chores at home.\n- It was only after that period ended that I crashed into this heavy, depressed state, which has dragged on for about nine months now without lifting.\n\nDoctor: During that two-month high-energy period, did you have any severe problems like needing to be hospitalized, losing touch with reality, or being unable to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but I absolutely lost touch with reality, hearing voices from the mountains and being convinced I was chosen for some special artistic mission. My ability to function completely fell apart during that time, and my studio work went into total disarray—I missed major grant deadlines, lost students, and my business partner almost walked away because of my erratic behavior.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you ever need to be hospitalized or did the symptoms make it impossible for you to function safely in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but my functioning was so derailed that a close friend eventually had to convince me to see a psychiatrist to save my studio. During that high period, I was doing things like driving to trailheads at midnight and completely neglecting major business deadlines, and my partner was ready to walk away. It was absolutely impossible for me to run my life or business safely and responsibly."
  }

It felt like this sudden, intense wave where the mountains were guiding my choreography, but those voices actually stopped once I crashed into this current low, heavy phase.\n\nDoctor: Since those experiences started, have you had any other periods like the revved-up, barely sleeping phase, or have the unusual voices and messages continued even when your mood is not elevated or low?\n\nPatient: No, those voices and messages completely stopped once I crashed, and they haven't come back at all. I also haven't had another one of those revved-up phases since then. I've just been stuck in this really heavy, low place for the last several months.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week where you felt unusually energized or irritable, needed very little sleep, talked more than usual, had racing thoughts, or did risky things, even if you did not hear voices then?\n\nPatient: Yes, I had a highly energized period just like that when I first launched the studio earlier this year. I felt completely unstoppable, barely slept for weeks, talked constantly about my ideas, and was incredibly active. But I actually did hear those voices during that exact same high-energy phase, not separate from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had hallucinations or unusual beliefs when your mood was not high or low, or do they only happen during those energized or depressed periods?\n\nPatient: No, they have never happened when my mood was normal. The voices and the feeling of having a special mission only occurred during that highly energized, revved-up period when I wasn't sleeping. Once I crashed into this low, depressed phase, those experiences stopped completely.\n\nDoctor: How long did that revved-up, energized period last—was it at least 1 week, or did it go on for 4 days or less?\n\nPatient: Oh, it lasted much longer than just a week or a few days. It actually went on for about two months of constant, intense activity before I finally hit a wall and crashed. It was weeks and weeks of feeling completely wired and barely sleeping.\n\nDoctor: During that two-month energized period, did you also have any times of feeling very depressed or losing interest in things for at least two weeks, or was it only the high-energy phase followed by the current low phase?\n\nPatient: No, those first two months were purely that high-energy, unstoppable phase. It was only after that period ended that I crashed into this heavy, depressed state, which has dragged on for about nine months now without lifting.\n\nDoctor: During that two-month high-energy period, did you have any severe problems like needing to be hospitalized, losing touch with reality, or being unable to function at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but I absolutely lost touch with reality, hearing voices from the mountains and being convinced I was chosen for some special artistic mission. My ability to function completely fell apart during that time, and my studio work went into total disarray—I missed major grant deadlines, lost students, and my business partner almost walked away because of my erratic behavior.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you ever need to be hospitalized or did the symptoms make it impossible for you to function safely in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but my functioning was so derailed that a close friend eventually had to convince me to see a psychiatrist to save my studio. During that high period, I was doing things like driving to trailheads at midnight and completely neglecting major business deadlines, and my partner was ready to walk away. It was absolutely impossible for me to run my life or business safely and responsibly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a major depressive episode before this one, or is this the first time you’ve had a long low period like this?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced a low period like this before. This is the first time I've ever felt this heavy, exhausted, and completely unable to function."
  }
]

The psychotic experiences occurred only during the elevated phase and resolved when the mood shifted, which makes a primary psychotic disorder less likely and keeps the bipolar-spectrum code as the leading candidate.","is_final":false}

What began as her characteristic last-minute planning became a sustained period of increasingly extreme schedules—late-night rehearsals, spontaneous road trips to scout outdoor sites, and impulsive commitments to multiple festivals—leaving her studio finances and class structure in disarray. Friends and collaborators grew alarmed when she started speaking about receiving special messages from the mountains guiding her work and making studio decisions based on these experiences, while she stopped responding to emails from venues and missed grant deadlines, jeopardizing her reputation. After two months in which her business partner threatened to dissolve their agreement, students dropped out over erratic class times, and she then swung into a demoralized spell where she cancelled workshops and stayed home, a close friend finally convinced her to see a psychiatrist by framing it as getting support to save her studio and make sense of the unsettling experiences she was having on be wrong about.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): When I’m out on the trails—or sometimes even just walking down Main Street—I hear this low, clear voice coming from the ridgelines or the buildings, telling certain places because I’m scared of what it will tell me next.\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): After those weeks of feeling unstoppable, I’ve crashed into this heavy, gray place where I wake up with my really knowing why.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): At first, during the high period, I was barely eating because I was too wired and ‘busy changing the dance world,’ and then, when I crashed, my appetite swung the other way and I started mindlessly eating cereal or takeout straight from the container because cooking or even thinking about real food feels like too much work.\n- S027 awake all night replaying every mistake I’ve made with the studio or sleeping until the afternoon and still waking up feeling like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My body has slowly that even walking from my bed to the kitchen feels like I’m dragging through wet cement.\n- S029 (Fatigue): Even on days when I don’t teach or rehearse, I feel utterly wiped out, like my muscles are made of sandbags and my brain is fogged over, and just answering a simple text from a student about class times can exhaust me enough that I have to lie back down.\n- S030 who ruined everyone’s chances at something good, and I lie there going over every impulsive choice I made and feeling like I deserve to lose everything.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): It’s like I’ve been riding this wild swing where one week I’m so amped up and euphoric that I’m blasting music at 3 a.m., planning ten new projects and talking a mile a minute about my ‘movement revolution,’ they’ve checked out.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): When I’m wound up, my attention is all over the place—one second I’m trying to finalize the studio’s class schedule, then I hear a car horn or see a shadow on the wall and suddenly I’m off reworking a lighting plot or rewriting an email in my head, and nothing actually gets finished because every little sound or movement yanks me in a new direction.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from a manageable teaching load to cramming my days and nights with extra classes, pop-up workshops, new performance pieces, and constant social media promotion, running around town to meet with anyone who would listen, yet at the same time pacing the studio floor for hours in between because even when there was nothing concrete to do I felt My ability to function completely fell apart during that time, and my studio work went into total disarray—I missed major grant deadlines, lost students, and my business partner almost walked away because of my erratic behavior.\n\nDoctor: During that high-energy period, did you ever need to be hospitalized or did the symptoms make it impossible for you to function safely in daily life?\n\nPatient: I wasn't hospitalized, but my functioning was so derailed that a close friend eventually had to convince me to see a psychiatrist to save my studio. During that high period, I was doing things like driving to trailheads at midnight and completely neglecting major business deadlines, and my partner was ready to walk away. It was absolutely impossible for me to run my life or business safely and responsibly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a major depressive episode before this one, or is this the first time you’ve had a long low period like this?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced a low period like this before. This is the first time I've ever felt this heavy, exhausted, and completely unable to function.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any similar episodes of unusually high energy, little need for sleep, or feeling ‘wired’ before this year, even if they did not include hallucinations?"
  }

Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.
